    Changelog¶

    The one changelog of the project, published as the Changelog page of the documentation site. Each entry is a dated heading followed by bullets that state what changed and where; the newest entry stands first. An entry of the release names the lane whose work it records, and an entry of the work before the release names its subject. The entries of the release 0.01 stand under its heading, and the entries of the work before it under the heading "Before the release".

    After the release 0.04¶

    2026-11-14 — The refusal of a control defined by itself gives its reason alone¶

    • A line of a backward block that defines a control by itself, c = c, is refused under definition-order with its reason, and the refusal names no marked spelling to write (AAS, 14 November 2026). The sentence of 13 November advised c = c[>] with c[>] listed in the header, and the header of such a block refuses a marked control under field-perch.
    • The sentence for a value-type field, L = L, is unchanged: it still names L = L[>] and L[<] = ….

    2026-11-14 — A method's entry in a methods file writes no number¶

    • A methods file no longer writes a number among the arguments of a method word. Every number that a method reads is a setting, declared in the methods file and valued in the settings file, as the bounds and the counts of a grid already were (AAS, 14 November 2026). An entry such as !gauss_hermite(n_ψ=7, n_ξ=7) is refused where it is bound, under the new rule method-setting-declared, at any depth of a tuple or of a word's own arguments. It is written !gauss_hermite(n_ψ=n_ψ_nodes, n_ξ=n_ξ_nodes), with n_ψ_nodes : ℕ and n_ξ_nodes : ℕ declared in the methods file and the two counts given in the settings file.
    • stage.settings therefore lists every number that a method or a grid reads, and two versions of a stage that differ only in such a number have the same methods and differ in their configuration.
    • The grammar is unchanged: it reads the number, and the refusal is made where the entry is bound, as for a number in a grid entry. A quoted string in a method's entry remains admitted and is no setting.
    • No stage file or methods file under applications/ or tutorials/ wrote the form.
    • The rule revises the sentence of Appendix D §4 on the value of a keyword argument. Methods §§4 and 6, Appendix E.4, Appendix G, the abstract grammar's requirements on a typing entry, Methodization §§3.1, 4 and 7, the reference of the method types and the wiki's entries on methods and settings state it.

    2026-11-13 — Eleven corrections where a stage writes a second value-type field¶

    Each correction has a test, and no other test of the package changes its outcome. A value-type field is one that a header lists with the role word !vlu, as the value V is.

    • The card of a builder, the text that denote() prints, gives under each output of an Aggregation the line that writes it. With the lines of a value block in another order than its header lists the outputs, L = L[>] before V = 𝑢(c) + β*V[>] under (V, L), the card printed each rule under the other output. Where a block began with a local definition, the card printed that definition as the rule of the value. The kernel was right in both cases and is unchanged.
    • A derivative line, D_{m}V = 𝑑𝑢(c), is recorded on the value that its left side names. In a block that writes two value-type fields the card printed the line under both, and value_carries_derivative answered that the second field carried the derivative.
    • bellman.trellis.derivative_missing reads the derivative of an arrival value through an arrival block that also writes another field, Θ[<] = Θ beside V[<] = V. Before, a second field in a terminal stage made it report the derivative readings of the stages before it as unmet.
    • A value-type field that the feasible set of a policy line reads, as L[>] in Γ_c(x : ℝ₊₊) := {c : ℝ₊₊ | x - c >= L[>]}, is an input of the policy builder. Its signature lists the field, the route from the next period requires it, and stage.diagnostics() records no unread-input for the listing. Before, the signature listed the value alone, and the route into a period that supplies no such field was certified.
    • The connection report decides a named set written with one condition that compares its variable with a bound, Xa := {x : ℝ | x > 0} against Xk := {x : ℝ | x > -0.3}, as it decides the intervals (0, inf) and (-0.3, inf). It reads a bound that is a derived parameter, j := l - 0.25, at its value. Where it leaves a comparison undecided, its sentence gives the reason, and it no longer names the variable of a condition as a bound.
    • compile_kernel() compiles max and min on a line of a block, L = max(L[>], b), to the greater and the lesser of the arguments, by the rule that writes them in the definition of a derived parameter (Expression forms §3). Before, the kernel of the whole Aggregation was refused under kernel-form, the component of the value with it.
    • In a stage whose label is chosen, the check behind branch-tuple-mismatch takes as the value line the line whose tuple the policy maximizes. A second field that the label selects, Θ = (Θ_w[>], Θ_r[>])[d], written after the value line made the stage be refused; the stage is now read in either order of the two lines.
    • A line of a backward block whose two sides are one bare name, L = L, is refused under definition-order, as the same line of a forward block is. The sentence names the marks that tell the two fields apart, L = L[>] and L[<] = ….
    • A named set or a declared function that reads bare a field which the headers list with a mark alone, Xm := {x : ℝ | x > L} where a header lists L[>], is refused under name-in-scope, with the file, the line and the marked spelling. Before, the elaboration raised a Python KeyError.
    • The refusal under shock-observed of an arrival line with no expectation advises the expectation over every shock that the edge draws, E_{ψ,ξ}(…). Before, it advised the first shock alone, and the line written as advised was refused again.
    • Where a block of a methods file typed !explicit stands in place of a stage's block and writes no line for an output that the stage's header lists, the refusal under output-defined names the block of the methods file and says that its lines stand in place of the stage's. Before, it pointed at the block of the stage file, which writes the line.

    2026-11-12 — A selection by one label carries a value NumPy cannot convert¶

    • The text of a compiled kernel writes a selection by a label, (θ, ξ_u)[j], through an inner function, where_label, in place of np.where. Every operand NumPy takes gives what np.where gave, at one label or at each point of an array of labels. Where np.where refuses an operand it cannot convert, as it refuses a value that JAX is tracing, and the key is one label, the function returns the component the label selects at the common shape of the operands, the shape np.where gives its result, so that a caller can trace such a kernel with the label fixed, map it over quadrature nodes or differentiate it; before, JAX was refused at np.where. A selection by an integer key is written with np.where as before, and Numba compiles it as before (packages/bellman/tests/test_finite_indexed_data.py).

    2026-11-12 — Calibration refuses a faulty value at its own name before a definition computes with it¶

    • calibrate judges each value against its own declared set before it evaluates a definition of a derived parameter that reads the value. With ξ_u : ℝ₊ @ [0, ξ_u_sup), ξ_u_sup := 1/p_zero, s := (1 - p_zero*ξ_u)/(1 - p_zero) and μ := log(s), a value of ξ_u at or above 1/p_zero is refused at ξ_u, with the condition ξ_u < ξ_u_sup and the value of the bound. Before, the three definitions were evaluated first, and the calibration was refused under derived-parameter at μ, whose logarithm has no value there.
    • The same holds where a second parameter's set reads a derived parameter computed from the faulty value, as a_Art : (-inf, 0] @ (a_Nat, inf) does where a_Nat is defined from ξ_u: the calibration is refused at ξ_u in whatever order the file declares the two. Before, the first of the two in the file was named.
    • A derived value outside the set its definition declares is refused at that definition before another definition reads it: with q : (0, 1) := p_zero*ξ_u and h := 1/(1 - q), a value of q of one is refused at q, and no longer at the division by zero in h.
    • The derived parameter that a set reads is valued before the set is judged, wherever the file defines it. A parameter and a derived parameter that read each other, x : ℝ @ (y, inf) with y := 2*x, or through a further definition, x : (0, z) with y := x + 1 and z := 2*y, are judged as before, after the definitions have their values.
    • The values that the set of a value reads are judged with the value, and a set written by name, Xu := [0, ξ_u_sup) with ξ_u : Xu, is read through its definition. With a second parameter ξ_mid : (ξ_u, ξ_u_sup) and a definition that reads ξ_mid alone, a value of ξ_u at or above its bound is refused at ξ_u, and not at ξ_mid. A derived value outside the set its definition declares is refused at that definition, and not at a parameter whose set reads it.
    • The values at which a stage calibrates are unchanged, and so are the values of the derived parameters. A calibration fails the same conditions as before; the change is in which of them the refusal names.

    2026-11-12 — The agents' rule on parameters and settings¶

    • AGENTS.md gains a rule of usage after "The model and its numerics are kept apart": "parameter" names an input of the mathematical model and "setting" an input of a numerical method, in documentation, comments, docstrings, names, commit messages and chat, also where another toolkit's documentation calls a method's input a parameter. The two words are defined in the concept wiki and in Appendix E.4 of the Bellman calculus; that the word "parameter" is kept for the quantities that define the economy was stated until now only on the wiki's page on functional forms, where it concerns the arguments of a form (CDC, 6 November 2026).

    2026-11-12 — min, max and a definition by cases in a derived parameter, and one reading of an infinite endpoint¶

    • The definition of a derived parameter, a constant that a stage file or a methods file defines from its parameters by :=, may call min and max, each of one argument or more, beside log and exp, as in min(R, Γ). Calibration evaluates every argument and takes the least or the greatest. A call of any other function is refused under derived-parameter, or under not-constructed where that call is the whole right side, as in h := sqrt(R), and a call of one of the four where the stage defines a function of that name is refused under derived-parameter (Equation parsing rules §2).
    • A derived parameter may be defined by cases. The right side of its definition is then a conditional, if test then a else b, as in h := if Γ < R then 1/(1 - Γ/R) else 0, and the definition may declare a set before := as any definition of a derived parameter may. The test compares two formulas by <, <=, =, >= or >, with ≤ and ≥ as second spellings of <= and >=, and each of the two branches a and b is a formula, a further conditional, inf or -inf. The value is the branch that the test chooses, and the other branch is not evaluated, so that a division it writes is not made. A conditional stands as the whole right side of a definition without arguments and as a branch of another conditional. Written inside a formula, in a function's body or in a line of a block, it is refused under syntax, and the refusal says where the form stands.
    • A branch written inf or -inf gives that infinity as the derived parameter's value. Such a branch is admitted only where the declared set of the definition, the set written before := in which its value must lie, holds that infinity: a set of extended reals, the real numbers with an infinity adjoined, as in h : ℝ_{+∞} := if Γ < R then 1/(1 - Γ/R) else inf, or an interval closed at the infinite endpoint, such as (0, inf]. A definition that writes an infinite branch with no declared set, or with a set that by its form cannot hold the infinity, such as (0, inf) or ℝ̄ @ (0, inf), is refused under derived-parameter when the file is read, and the refusal gives a set to write. At a calibration the value, finite or infinite, is judged against the declared set, and stage.par prints it.
    • if, then and else are reserved words of the language. A file that writes one of them as a name, in a declaration, a definition, a function's argument, a block's header or a formula, is refused under syntax.
    • An endpoint of a declared set that is the bare name, the name with no arithmetic, of a parameter or of a derived parameter valued at an infinity is read as that infinity: with debt : ℝ₊ @ [0, h), the set of debt is [0, ∞) where h is inf. A bound that read a parameter declared on the extended reals at an infinite value, as debt : [0, T) reads T : ℝ̄ at T = inf, was left open before, with the membership listed as required by stage.obligations(), and is decided now. calibrate also decides whether a number lies in a set of extended reals, which it left required before: a finite number lies in every such set, and an infinity in those that hold it, so that inf is refused for a parameter declared on ℝ_{-∞} (Symbols and conventions §9.2). In a calibration file an infinite value is written .inf or -.inf, which the package reads as a number; it reads inf there as a text, and the refusal of a declaration that carries the value, T : ℝ̄ := inf, advises T: .inf, where it advised T: inf before.
    • A bound of a declared set, an endpoint or a second set written after @, may call min and max as it may call log and exp. calibrate decides the membership of a value in a set such as β : ℝ₊₊ @ (0, min(R, 2)), which it left required before, and configure refuses a setting's value outside such a set, which it bound before without a check.
    • No arithmetic of infinities is defined. A definition that evaluates a name valued at an infinity, in a formula, in a test, in a call or as the bare name, has no value, and the calibration is refused at that definition. A bound that computes with such a name, as 2*T does at T = inf, or with the written infinity, as 2*inf does at every calibration, has no value either, and the calibration is refused at that bound; before, the membership was left required and the value was bound.
    • A compiled kernel, the Python function that compile_kernel() answers for an operation of a stage (Expression forms §3), writes the min and max of a derived parameter that it reads as np.minimum and np.maximum, and a definition by cases as a call of a function. Where the test is one truth value, that function evaluates the chosen branch alone. Where the test is an array, as when the kernel is called with an array of values of a parameter, the function takes at each entry the value of the branch that the test chooses there, and a branch neither raises nor warns at the entries where the test does not choose it. The same selection is made where the kernel's text is run with JAX's arrays and compiled by jax.jit: the compiler calls the function with placeholders in place of the numbers, so that the test has no one truth value, and both branches are evaluated.

    2026-11-11 — A grid bound over a set whose endpoint is a parameter¶

    • configure decides whether a grid's bound lies in the coordinate's declared set at the calibration that is bound, where the set reads a parameter: with Xa := [a_Art, inf) and a borrowing limit a_Art of zero, a savings grid from 0.001 to 20 configures. Earlier every such bound was refused under grid-bounds as "not decidably inside" the set, although the declared set of a setting was already read at the calibration; one value now receives one answer from both checks (Methodization §3.2).
    • A bound outside the set at the calibration is refused under grid-bounds, and the sentence names the parameter's value: "which lies outside the declared set Xa of a at a_Art = 0.0". The points of !Points and the nodes of a constructed grid are judged in the same way, so a declared point outside its set at the calibration, which was admitted undecided, is refused.
    • Before a calibration is bound nothing decides such a membership, and a bound over such a set is refused as before, so calibrate comes before configure for a grid on a set that reads a parameter. A set that reads no parameter is decided without a calibration, as before.

    0.04, the internal developer release¶

    • The fourth internal release gathers the work on main since the tag internal-dev-0.03, whose dated entries stand below. Its principal parts are several sets in one type, written with @, with calibrate and configure deciding a set with conditions; a union and a difference of sets of numbers, and a declared set on a derived parameter, by which a condition on several parameters is stated; Appendix D of the calculus as the one account of the types, with the reference pages Rules and Types printed from tables of the package; the rule that, at a connection, the declared set of the outgoing field lies in that of the incoming field; and the homepage's type, spacing and layout.
    • The release is the tag internal-dev-0.04 of 12 November 2026 on main, at the commit that records it. On that commit, checked in a clean copy of the repository, mkdocs build --strict reported no warning and the check of the package's imports found no fault. The package's suite gave 1,231 tests passed, 24 skipped and 10 failed. The ten are those that failed at the release 0.03: they test the documentation's folders and navigation, the wiki's links, the branching examples (five tests), the trellis of the factory, a connector's qualified names and the applications' folder layout. The checks of the document properties and of the decision records reported 67 and 65 faults, against 69 and 65 at the release 0.03.

    2026-11-12 — A union and a difference of sets, a condition on a derived parameter, and one account of the types¶

    • A declared set may be a union or a difference of sets of numbers, written with \/ and \, with ∪ and ∖ read as second spellings: ρ : ℝ₊₊ \ {1} declares a positive parameter other than one, and ρ : (0,1) \/ (1, inf) declares the same set. The product binds before the two signs and the arrow after them. The two forms are read in the set of a parameter, of a derived parameter and of a setting, and are refused elsewhere under readable-form (Symbols and conventions §9.2). calibrate and configure decide whether a value lies in a union or in a difference, and leave the membership required only where no member decides it.
    • A derived parameter may carry a declared set on its definition, q : (0,1) := (β*R)^(1/ρ)/R, and calibrate then refuses a calibration at which the value of the formula lies outside the set. A condition on several parameters at once is stated in this way, as the set of a named formula of them. The table stage.par holds the set and the definition of such a name.
    • Corrections. Two different perch words in one type are refused. Several sets with no number in common are refused, also where their endpoints are ratios of two integers, as in (0,1) @ (1/2, 1/3); sets written alike count once. One interval that holds no number, (2, 1), and a difference that holds no number, (0,1) \ ℝ₊, are refused. A line of a block that writes a parameter, a setting or a derived parameter is refused. The set of a parameter, of a setting or of a derived parameter that reads a field is refused, whether it reads the field directly, through a named set or through the law of a shock. calibrate refuses a calibration at which a bound has no value, and it values a bound that calls log or exp.
    • Nine example stage files state in their declarations what they stated in words. The Epstein–Zin savings stage under applications/epstein-zin-savings declares β : (0,1) and the return patience factor at the certainty-equivalent return, q : (0,1) := (β*exp((1-ρ)*(μ_Ψ + (1-γ)*σ_Ψ^2/2)))^(1/ρ), in place of a bound that held an expectation and that calibration never decided. The consumption-savings stage with i.i.d. income declares γ : (1, inf), and seven stages exclude the value one of a curvature parameter with a difference of sets. The perfect-foresight example also writes the return impatience condition on the definition of its return patience factor, q : (0,1) := (β*R)^(1/ρ)/R, beside the bound of its discount factor.
    • Appendix D of the calculus is rewritten as the one account of the types: the words type, sort, kind and carrier; the admissible set of the parameters, to which each declaration and each typed definition adds one condition; the state signature and the operator signature of a stage; its forward and its backward model; and the Bellman operator as the arrival builder applied after the optimizing decision builder, at each version of a stage. Its paragraph on periods states the rule for a connection between stages that the entry of 11 November records. The other pages of the calculus, its catalogues and the wiki are brought into agreement with it.
    • Appendix C of Bellman-SYM states, rule by rule, what the package does, and lists in its §5.3 the rules that the package does not yet check. Two reference pages are printed from tables of the package: Rules, which gives for each rule the functions that implement and check it, the refusals it raises and one test, and Types, which lists the objects that a file types, the forms of a set and the limits of the language.
    • The package bellman is version 0.1.7. The lessons and the application notebooks that read a changed stage file, or that print the version, are executed again; their source cells are unchanged.

    2026-11-11 — Compatible declared sets at a connection¶

    • Period Syntax §2 defines compatible declared sets at a connection: the set of the outgoing field is contained in the set of the incoming field, and the two sets need not be equal, since a declared set states where a field's values may lie and does not claim that every member is reached. Trellis Syntax §2 points to the definition. The check the package makes is unchanged.
    • Stage composition §1 states the same rule in the terms of Appendix D, whose remarks record that a connection uses domain refinement, the continuation field's domain contained in the arrival field's, and not equality of domains.

    2026-11-11 — Homepage type, spacing and layout¶

    • The homepage sets its text in five sizes, stated for a window 1440 px wide: 96 px for the headline, 56 px for section headings, 24 px for titles and statements, 16 px for text, links and buttons, and 12 px for labels in capitals. Its distances are taken from eight steps between 4 px and 96 px, and every section stands on one grid of twelve columns. The sizes are stated at the head of docs/_stylesheets/home.css.
    • The opening section shows the two triangles as one drawing at the left; the headline, the standfirst and the two buttons start on the centre line. The column of the lower triangle runs to its top corner, the top bar meets the column's side, and the orange block is the column's lowest block. The standfirst reads "A declarative, modular and machine verifiable language system for dynamic economics."
    • The three tiles of the second section are Bellman language, Semantic ontology and AI interface, each with a new description.
    • Applications shows Housing, Pensions and Buffer-stock saving as three tabs in a row above the model, which takes the full width of the page. The sentence that described each model and its link to the example page are removed; the links to all examples and to the Workbench guide remain. The left and right arrow keys move between the tabs, as the up and down keys do.
    • The diagram of a model, which stood at the upper left of the Bellman semantics pane, stands in the middle of the pane.
    • GitHub stands alone at the right-hand end of the header, after a menu that ends with AI. The footer has a Contribute link beside About us, Docs and GitHub.
    • In a window of 767 px or less the drawing stands above the headline, and the tabs take a second and a third row where the window is too narrow for one.

    2026-11-11 — Several sets in one type¶

    • A declaration may write several sets in one type, with @ between them, and the declared name then lies in each of them: β : (0,1) @ (0, R^(ρ-1)) declares a discount factor below one and below R^(ρ-1). A bound is an interval, whose endpoints may name parameters and whose unbounded side is -inf or inf, as in θ : ℤ @ [1, inf). At most one of the sets is written otherwise than as an interval, the sets may stand in any order, and the form is read on the declaration of one name on its own line (Symbols and conventions §9.2).
    • The earlier spelling of a bound, a condition after a comma as in β : (0,1), < R^(ρ-1), is retired. A file that writes it is refused under syntax, and the refusal gives the sets to write.
    • calibrate decides whether a value lies in a set with conditions: the several sets of a type, and a named set written with a bar such as Xb := {x : (0,1) | x < R}. It refuses a value that breaks a bound and names the bound and the values it read, and configure does the same for a setting. Earlier every value of such a set was accepted, a value outside its base included. Where a set reads another parameter, as a bound or as an endpoint, a faulty value is refused at its own parameter, and not at the parameter whose set reads it, in whatever order the file declares the two. A bound that reads a random variable or a derived parameter is not decided and remains a stated requirement.
    • The parameters line of a view and stage.par print a declared set as its base followed by one interval for each bound, β : (0, 1) @ (0, R^(ρ - 1)). The set does not depend on the order of the sets written or on how their endpoints are grouped into intervals, and a declaration that writes the printed text declares the same set. The stage sheet prints a named set with a bar as its definition writes it.
    • The perfect-foresight example states the return impatience condition as the upper bound of its discount factor, beside the finite human wealth condition on its growth factor, and calibration refuses a calibration that breaks either, whichever methods file is bound. Its notebook is executed again.
    • The smooth-ambiguity example declares η : (1,inf) @ [γ, inf). The bound was strict, which excluded the ambiguity-neutral calibration η = γ that the stage's own checks use. The Epstein–Zin stage, one stage of the test corpus and twelve fixtures of the tests of Bellman Workbench and of the comparator are rewritten in the new spelling with their sets unchanged.

    0.03, the internal developer release¶

    • The third internal release gathers the work on main since the tag internal-dev-0.02, whose dated entries stand below. Its principal parts are Bellman Workbench with its desktop application and its guide; the trellis, the period file and the recipe, with parameters shared within a period and connections between stages; the Matsya client and its service; finite index sets, named integer domains, further continuous shock distributions and terminal continuation values in the language; and the methods file as an addition beside the stage file, with method blocks and marginal blocks, grids, representation families, explicit solutions and derived parameters.
    • The release is the tag internal-dev-0.03 of 10 November 2026 on main, at the commit that records it. On that commit, checked in a clean copy of the repository, mkdocs build --strict reported no warning and the check of the package's imports found no fault. The package's suite gave 1,175 tests passed, 25 skipped and 10 failed. The ten, which also failed before the day's entries, test the documentation's folders and navigation, the wiki's links, the branching examples (five tests), the trellis of the factory, a connector's qualified names and the applications' folder layout. The checks of the document properties and of the decision records reported 69 and 65 faults, the numbers they held before the day's entries.

    2026-11-10 — The Workbench guide¶

    • The Workbench guide gives task instructions for Bellman Workbench in nine pages, under a section of their own in the navigation: a quickstart on the supplied portfolio model, building a model with Matsya, opening and editing a project, inspecting a model, editing equations and inputs, creating and connecting stages, managing recipes and variants, and sharing a project and finding the cause of a problem.
    • Install and Bellman Workbench point to the guide. The overview page's account of the Matsya column is replaced by a pointer to Build a model with Matsya, and the supplied example of the install page is applications/cons-port.
    • The navigation lists the example page of the perfect-foresight consumption model, Perfect Foresight (CRRA), under Examples.

    2026-11-10 — Grids, function representations and explicit solutions¶

    • A methods file types a grid with !Cartesian, !Points or !Fibred. !Points(nodes=P) takes the points of P, a setting declared on a fixed finite product of points or a set defined by its members, in the declared order of the place's coordinates; !Points alone is the empty grid. !Fibred(base=!Points(nodes=B), fibre=!Cartesian(c=(Γ_c, n_c))) pairs each state of the base with n_c evenly spaced points of the feasible interval Γ_c at that state, endpoints included; it is admitted where the domain of the place is the product of those states and the control, as at a !vfi builder. Each constructor may end with require= and a predicate word, !Empty, !Cartesian, !NonCartesian or !UniformCartesian, or a tuple of them, which states a condition on the resulting grid; an unknown or contradictory word is refused where it is written (Method types; Methods §5).
    • configure refuses a grid that fails a requirement its bound values decide, under grid-requirement. Once the settings are bound, kernels() of a builder answers under grids the realized grid of each place within it that types a grid, with its constructor, its coordinates and its finite points, repeated points removed. Where the points or a required property cannot be established, as for an omitted factor, an open feasible interval or a fibre of two controls, whose joint sampling is unsupported, the entry is the refusal that says why, and no default is supplied. same_grid of bellman.stage.grids compares two realized grids on the same coordinates exactly, with no tolerance (A builder §5).
    • !Linear, !pchip and the closed form, written !closed_form, are representation families registered with the package. register, unregister and families of bellman.stage.representations add, remove and list families. A RepresentationFamily states its typed arguments, the data sources it accepts, its derivatives, its stored data and whether it reads a grid, and supplies no evaluator. A family registered after the package is imported may be named in a methods file, and a duplicate word or the word of another method is refused. A stage keeps the families it was bound with, so that a later registration or removal changes only later bindings.
    • The record of each representation in stage.methods names its data source under source. The source is selected from all the methods files the stage holds, taken together, in a fixed order: an authored table evaluate(knots, values, x), then the policy pairs that !egm selects at the closing Evaluation of its builder, then a complete authored expression, such as the lines of an !explicit builder, and otherwise the family's own nodes on its grid. !Linear and !pchip accept a table, the pairs or their own nodes; the closed form accepts an expression and refuses a grid. A source the family does not accept is refused under representation-source, and no other source is chosen in its place; at the closing Evaluation of an !egm builder, a family that reads the pairs refuses a grid of its own. A grid beside an authored table holds exactly the table's knots: configure compares the two supports with no sorting and no tolerance, the two signed zeros being one point, and a support not yet known leaves the obligation table-grid-support in stage.obligations() (Methods and places).
    • !explicit types a policy builder or a backward value builder whose solution is known in closed form. Its block writes one line output = expression for each output, which may read the states and controls of its output perch, the parameters, the derived parameters and the names that earlier lines define. The lines replace the maximization at the operative level, stage.declared keeps it, and the compiled kernel of each line evaluates its output. A line that reads a value input or its derivative, states a relation, applies argmax, solve or upper_env, or reads a field at another perch is refused under explicit-solution, and the same block typed !egm or !time_iteration is refused with the advice to type !explicit. Unless a representation is written at the builder's Evaluation, the Evaluation receives !closed_form, which stage.methods and the signature show with the entry it comes from, such as generated from policy.method; removing !explicit removes it (Methodization).
    • A stage file or a methods addition defines a derived parameter with := from declared parameters, numbers and earlier derived parameters, using arithmetic, powers, log and exp, such as h := 1/(1 - Γ/R). The calibration supplies no value for it. calibrate, and methodize on a calibrated stage, evaluate the definitions before the checks of the distributions and their supports, so that a distribution whose scale is a negative derived value is refused under distribution-domain. A cycle among the definitions, a name that is not a parameter and a call other than log and exp are refused at elaboration. A calibration that gives a derived parameter a value, or at which a definition has no finite real value, is refused at the definition under derived-parameter. stage.par and denote() show each definition and, once the stage is calibrated, its value; the stored calibration keeps the declared parameters alone (Parameters and settings §1.1).
    • The new application consumption-perfect-foresight-crra, the perfect-foresight consumption model with utility of constant relative risk aversion, is solved in two ways from one stage file, which defines the return patience factor q, human wealth h and the natural borrowing limit L as derived parameters, with one calibration file (Perfect foresight consumption with CRRA utility). Its methods file methods.bl defines the marginal propensity to consume κ := 1 - q and types the policy builder and both value builders !explicit, with the closed-form consumption function and values; the replacing selection methods.egm.bl solves the stage by the endogenous grid method on a grid of savings, with !Linear at the policy builder's Evaluation. The application's module compares the two methods and evaluates the value functions with scalar Linear and PCHIP evaluators, which refuse a query outside the interval of their nodes unless the comparison of policies explicitly extends the Linear evaluator along its first and last segments, and each use of that extension is reported.
    • In the buffer-stock application, the log-mean of the positive transitory draw is the derived parameter μ_ξ := -log(1 - p_zero) - σ_ξ^2/2, so that E[ξ] = 1 holds at every calibration, and a calibration that supplies a value for μ_ξ is refused under derived-parameter. At σ_ξ = 0 the positive draw is the point mass at 1/(1 − p_zero) (Buffer Stock Consumption Model).
    • Method types is generated from the records that the checks of a methods file read. Each method, each registered representation family and each grid constructor has one entry, which states its meaning, its declaration and the places where it may be written, its type parameters, its arguments and the meaning of their omission, the grids or data it accepts, an example, its admission and whether a numerical implementation is identified, and its links. An index of the places at which each method may stand, by operator, defined function, line and builder, precedes the method entries, and a table of the grid constructors and a table of the predicate words of require= precede the grid entries. An entry with no registered arguments says that unlisted keywords are retained for the numerical handler.

    2026-11-10 — Evaluation and interpolation definitions¶

    • Fields, functions and types distinguishes applying a policy from Evaluation's identity on the retained function.
    • Methods states the admitted-data and interpolation conditions for nodal reproduction and idempotence. The change is mathematical documentation; numerical implementations are unchanged.

    2026-11-09 — Homepage examples and project guide¶

    • The opening section adopts the revised description, artwork crop, face lighting, corner triangle and button placement, while retaining the link to homepage examples.
    • The homepage retains its original header navigation and groups features, model examples and project components into separate sections. Housing, Pensions and Buffer-stock saving each show a native Workbench diagram and authored source in selectable views, with links to the model and Workbench guides.
    • The original Dalí cubes overlay the full-width market, rowing and annual night-light imagery in Main features, with uniform navy shading and no directional image fade. The three feature entries share one opaque square navy panel above it, without a section-heading row or overview link. The cubes retain their geometry and animation without a separate lower band. Scenes cycle automatically; scrolling changes the actual Earth year, shown in a small caption. Pause/Play replaces scene and year selectors, and video pauses during scrolling. The page remains scrollable over diagrams.

    2026-11-09 — Website loading and mobile layout¶

    • The homepage retains the real market and rowing footage. The opening illustration has two opposing triangles on the original ultramarine background.
    • On small screens, touch devices and with reduced motion selected, text follows the normal page layout and the videos display still images.
    • The mobile properties panel no longer shifts above its container. Decorative three-dimensional fields are hidden in the simpler layout.
    • Pages without mathematics no longer load MathJax, the equation display library. The website uses https://bellman.io/ as its canonical address.

    2026-11-09 — Method components and binding refusals¶

    • A grid entry without a method entry is validated on its own. Replacing a stage's methods checks retained setting values against the new declarations before changing the stage; a refused replacement preserves the earlier stage.
    • Signature equality and hashing include the method and grid components beside inputs and outputs. Unpacking a signature still returns its inputs and outputs.
    • bind_stage accepts an optional on_refusal(error, verb) conversion while retaining refusal strings by default. The Workbench uses the original exception to preserve its rule, position and correction, and groups the same binding refusal from the stage and trellis readings once.
    • Target refusals separate their condition from the correction. The stage library and folder chooser list stage declarations without treating their methods files as additional stages.
    • A bare name in a method argument must be a declared setting; quoted text remains a literal value. Finite sets containing true or false admit the corresponding Boolean settings and keep them distinct from numerical zero and one.
    • The buffer-stock example retains its authored grid for evaluating the policy, with declared bounds and point count. Housing-renting's consumption additions declare each numerical option separately, preserving their existing values.
    • Workbench removes outdated editor marks on editing and applies a diagnostic only to the file and text that were checked. Related source links select a range only when its checked version is known. Save, layout, desktop and Git failures retain their operation, explanation and retry action in the diagnostic details; a failed save preserves the edited text.

    2026-11-07 — The methods file as an addition beside the stage file, on the how-to and reference pages¶

    • Methodization is written anew on the package as merged. A stage's methods stand in its methods file, methods.bl beside the stage file, a .bl file whose blocks name the stage's blocks by their ids and add typing entries to their brackets and lines to their bodies, and which the package reads when load_stage, methodize or a recipe names it. Its entries type the method and the grid of an operator, policy.method : !egm and policy.grid : !Cartesian(a=(a_min, a_max, n_a)), and name its handler, policy.handler : "cons-port:egm"; the paths of the places, the name of an expectation, E_ψ_ξ for E_{ψ,ξ}, the kinds of place and the refusals are stated; the settings a method reads are declared in the methods file with their sets and valued in the settings file; a solution method's lines stand in place of the stage file's maximization at the operative level while the declared level keeps it; the rules of the union and the replacing selection are given. The page quotes the buffer-stock methods file as it stands, prints stage.methods, the policy builder's signature and stage.settings from the package, and ends with the steps by which an economist writes the file for a model.
    • Method types reads its printed table with the entry each row writes, the keyword arguments the package reads and the perch its grid covers, and gains §3 on the grid: the kind !Cartesian, the four shapes of a factor, the first with an optional spacing word, and the default where a place writes no grid. Expression forms §4 describes the blocks of a methods file in place of the block file, its row of D shows the first spelling of D as the package's printer writes it, and the prose of Refusals names the rules under which an entry of a methods file is refused; the addresses evaluate.value and evaluate.marginal, the word !marginals, the key block:, the entry on: and the identity table are gone from every page but the historical example pages, which the examples index marks as the earlier YAML design and which keep their YAML listings; the Matsya pages still name the methods.yml file the service writes today.
    • Parameters and settings prints stage.par before and after calibration and stage.settings with the set each setting is declared in and the place that reads it, and Methods and places prints the places with one Evaluation place for each stored object, cntn_to_dcsn.evaluate[V] and cntn_to_dcsn.evaluate[D_{m_d}V], stage.methods with the word, the grid and the handler typed at each place, the printed signatures with their components, and the policy builder at its two levels. The other pages of the stage object are printed again on the consumption stage of the portfolio application as it stands, whose method lines are in its methods file, and the API index gains the rows of typing_entries, write_typing_entry, card_sections and derivative_missing and prints the rows of a line marked !doc on the portfolio stage.
    • Trellises, The Bellman factory, Model directory layout, Branching, Constraints, Appendix C, Appendix D, the binding verbs and the stage factory of the API, the overview pages Semantics, Workflow, Bellman Workbench and Fields, evaluation and methods, and the wiki entry specified stage quote the applications' methods files as merged and describe one methods.bl for each stage folder, a replacing selection methods.<variant>.bl beside it, a second file named after the first that adds to its selection, and the recipe row methods: {all: [stages/<key>/methods.bl]}; the branching page types a method at a branching stage by the paths of its places, a line inside a branch named under its label; the Workbench page describes the Settings tab with the declared set beside each value and the Methods tab's choice written into the methods file.
    • The documentation's printer covers every page of the language guide that holds a listing, the methodization page, the trellis page and the Bellman factory page among them, beside the API and reference pages.

    2026-11-05 — Homepage artwork and scrolling¶

    • The homepage keeps the original block grid and recession. Folded faces and tips share corner coordinates, and the narrowed tips fit inside their grid cells. The opening blocks hold across more of the scroll, briefly form a grid, then recede towards the horizon. Distant outlines fade, and the tips stay filled when the browser falls back to the static drawings. On touch and short screens the scenes scroll in document order with the text beneath the artwork.

    2026-11-05 — Methods as components of the signature, on the calculus pages and in the wiki¶

    • The Bellman calculus page Methods is written: an operator's signature carries its method and its grid as classifier components beside its inputs and outputs, each written as a typing of the operator's name, policy.method : !egm and policy.grid : !Cartesian(a=(a_min, a_max, n_a)); operators compose over their inputs and outputs alone and the labels of a composite are kept as a list; the method is a type over the kind of operator and takes keyword arguments; the grid is the discretized domain, a finite subset of the function's domain with its inclusion, over which a representation is a section; the settings a method reads are declared with their domains in the methods file and bound by configure; the handler is a binding beside the term and realizes the operator under Ρ; two erasures return the model, one forgetting the labels and one forgetting the methods file; a stage holds a declared and an operative level; nothing is differentiated. Functional programming §7 states the two erasures and the section law with the identity label on every operator without an entry, and Kinds and sorts §3 lists the grid as a sort of its own.
    • The concept wiki gains the entries grid, addition and methods, and settings is revised for the settings declared with their domains in the methods file.

    2026-11-04 — Workbench model editing¶

    • Start with an empty trellis and write the first stage from the recipe. Choose a period file to place that stage. In the Files tree, arrow keys navigate folders and Enter opens files.
    • A variant can copy its calibration, settings and method assignments. The Notebook action writes a separate inspection notebook for each recipe.
    • Problems distinguishes connection, binding and construction errors and links back to their sources. In a narrow window, switch between full-width panels. The desktop File menu returns to Welcome and its list of recent projects.

    2026-11-01 — The grid as a tagged setting¶

    • A representation names its basis as the method, !Linear for piecewise linear interpolation or !pchip for the shape-preserving cubic, and its grid under settings: the grid's kind is the tag on the value of the setting grid, and the grid's fields, orders and bounds, stand beside it, settings: {grid: !Cartesian, orders: {m: n_m}, bounds: {m: [m_min, m_max]}}. !Cartesian is a kind of grid and no longer a method; it admits the fields orders and bounds, both required, and, where written, spacing and nodes. The endogenous grid method names its exogenous grid of savings the same way under its own entry, in the states of the continuation perch, orders: {a: n_a}. A representation at the policy builder's Evaluation where !egm stands writes no grid, since the knots of its interpolant are the pairs the method stores, and a representation of an authored evaluate(knots, values, x) needs none (Methodization §3; Method types).
    • Three forms are refused, each in one sentence naming the form to write or the reason: !Cartesian written as a method, orders and bounds written beside a basis or beside !egm without the setting grid, and a grid written at the policy builder's Evaluation under !egm (Refusals). A value of grid that names no kind of grid, the grid's fields written inside the tag, and the key grid written beside method are refused by the check of the options. The loader of a methods file, load_methods of bellman.stage.sources, ends a tag at a comma or a closing bracket inside a flow mapping, so that the form above written on one line is read as it is read when written over several lines.
    • configure checks the fields of a grid by their kinds, those of the exogenous grid of !egm at the states of the continuation perch, and stage.settings gives in its column grid the kind of the grid of which a setting is a field. Every methods file of the applications and of the Workbench's test models writes the tagged form: the entries that wrote !Cartesian as their method write !Linear, the piecewise linear interpolation their routines carry out, and every !egm entry names its grid of savings; buffer-stock's settings file gains n_a: 960, a_min: 0.001 and a_max: 20.0, and consumption-savings-iid and housing-owner gain a settings file for their grid of savings. The portfolio–consumption tool reads its grid of savings from the !egm entry, and the reference solution is reproduced in both orders.

    2026-10-31 — The written place of a representation¶

    • A representation method is attached to the Evaluation of a builder, and the methods file writes that place by what the Evaluation evaluates: policy.evaluate for the policy function the policy builder stores, <block-id>.evaluate.value for the value function a backward value builder writes, and <block-id>.evaluate.marginal for the derivative of that value a derivative line states, in the stage file or in a marginal block. stage.places() lists these addresses after the lines of the policy builder and of each backward value builder, of the kind operator evaluate; a solution method such as !egm stands on the builder (Methodization §2; AAS, 31 October 2026).
    • Three forms are refused, each with a sentence naming the address to write: a representation named on a builder, on: policy with !Linear; a representation named on a derivative line, on: cntn_to_dcsn.envelope; and on: evaluate in a stage that writes no evaluate(…) (Refusals). An authored evaluate(knots, values, x) keeps its address <block-id>.<statement-id>.evaluate. The value and its stated derivative share one Evaluation, which holds one representation method.
    • Every methods file of the applications and of the Workbench's test models that wrote a retired form writes the place at the Evaluation and binds the methods it bound before, except buffer-stock's. Its interpolation of consumption, bound on 31 October at the authored evaluate(m[>], c[>], m) of the method block's last line, policy.cons_choice.evaluate, is bound at the policy builder's Evaluation, policy.evaluate. The block's last line is the identification c[~] = c[>], its grid line is named g_cntn_to_dcsn, and !Linear on the arrival builder is written at dcsn_to_arvl.evaluate.value and dcsn_to_arvl.evaluate.marginal, one method for the value and its stated derivative. The pages that quote these files quote them as they now stand, and cons-port reproduces its reference in both orders. The decisions are the file MB-X of specification 0.6, with the rows marked For AAS.

    2026-10-31 — One repository becomes three: the development repository, the public repository and the backup¶

    • The repository is renamed econ-ark/bellman-dev on GitHub and stays private and whole: the toolkit, the internal folder, the team's branches and pull requests and the history since 29 December 2024. A new public repository, econ-ark/bellman, holds the toolkit alone, packages/bellman, packages/asva, packages/bellman-workbench, packages/bellman-desktop, packages/bellman-matsya-client, the applications, the documentation, the lessons and the editor extension; every commit on its main is the public subset of one development commit, written by an export script of the development repository and named in the commit message, and its first commit is a root commit. The Matsya service and the measurement harness stay in the development repository. A pull request merged on the public repository is applied to the development repository as a patch and returns in the next export. The documentation site at econ-ark.github.io/bellman is served by the public repository. Decided by AAS on 31 October 2026.
    • The root test of the repository's housekeeping keeps every exported file free of internal paths: ten test docstrings and one comment of the Workbench's editor that cited internal records by path now cite them by name, and the check of the simple-set-expressions acceptance record moved from the package's tests to a root test that only the development repository runs.
    • .githooks/pre-commit, enabled once per clone by git config core.hooksPath .githooks, refuses a staged file over 5 MB or a text that looks like a credential.

    2026-10-31 — The buffer-stock application names its quadrature and its asset grid¶

    • A methods file may hold one entry of each kind on one target, a solution method, a representation and a marginal block, since each kind has its own place; !egm on policy and !Linear on policy now stand together in one file, the second at the Evaluation that closes the policy builder (Methodization §4). The buffer-stock application writes its interpolation as an operation: the last line of its method block reads the consumption function at the resources between the candidate pairs by evaluate, and !Linear is bound on that operator occurrence, policy.cons_choice.evaluate, as the published HARK solution interpolates its consumption function; neither the decision value nor its derivative takes a representation of its own.
    • The methods file of applications/buffer-stock/ binds !gauss-hermite on the arrival builder's expectation over the two income shocks, with the node counts the new settings file names, seven for each shock, and !Linear on the stated derivative of the arrival value, D_{k}V[<], represented on the grid of 960 asset points from 0.001 to 20; the counts and the grid follow the published HARK solution of the model in the QuantEcon community library, whose equiprobable discretization and exponentially nested grid the method table does not name (Methodization §4.1; Method types).

The explicit way of writing is not wanted (AAS, 28 October 2026): the stage without a portfolio choice is written the method way, its derivative line D_{k}V = E_{θ}(D_{m}V[>]*R) in a marginal block bound by !marginals; the five reference trellis files bind the folder's own stages, with the reference's handlers on the block route, and the forward law of the portfolio and no-portfolio stages carries !monte-carlo with the settings n_sim and sim_seed. applications/portfolio-choice/ stays in place, superseded and marked for deletion, its readers listed for the deletion's own step.

    2026-10-28 — The applications are written the method way, and the trivial arrival builders go¶

    • Every application's stage file states its values and its maximizations alone. A derivative line stands in a marginal block of a block file under declaration/methods/, bound on the value builder by an entry method: !marginals whose block names the file from the methods file's folder, ../../methods/<file> from a per-stage methods file; the inverse Euler, endogenous-grid, candidate-value and upper-envelope lines stand in a method block bound on the policy builder by !egm, and a first-order condition in one bound by !time-iteration. Line ids are kept, so a representation bound on a derivative line's id keeps its address. Rewritten so: buffer-stock, retirement-choice, consumption-savings-iid, benhabib-2019, housing-renting, pension-retirement, durables, housing-frictions and housing-owner.
    • The degenerate arrival builders, a forward builder whose only lines are identities between the arrival states and the decision states and a backward builder writing V[<] = V, are removed from the stages that had them; such a stage is entered at its decision perch, and a connector of a period or a trellis names its decision states. A terminal stage whose states are typed in its arrival block alone keeps it, and cagetti-denardi-2006 keeps its arrival builders, which its branching connectors name.
    • The package: a method block may call upper_env and evaluate; a !marginals entry may share its builder with another method's entry; a maximization binder is in scope in the feasible sets written after it; the model explorer draws reading ports at a stage's entry perch; three refusal examples follow the moved lines of consumption-savings-iid.
    • The cons-port demonstration's portfolio stage names its continuation state w in place of m_next, and portfolio-choice's block file moves to applications/portfolio-choice/declaration/methods/, since the test corpus reads every .bl file under declaration/stages/ as a stage file.
    • The pages and the lessons that quote these files quote them as they now stand.     2026-10-26 — Matsya's composer is a text area sized by its text or by a handle¶

    • The field of the Matsya column's composer is a text area: Enter sends the question, Shift+Enter begins a new line, text pasted into it keeps its line breaks, and a question of several lines is listed with its breaks. Empty, the field is one line of 26 px with the line centered in it; it grows with its text by 18 px a line to eight lines, 152 px, and then scrolls (The Workbench §5).
    • The top edge of the composer's box is a handle: a drag upward enlarges the field while the conversation gives up the space down to 80 px, a drag downward stops at one line, a double click returns the field to the height of its text, and the browser keeps the height dragged with the window's other sizes for the folder, writing nothing in the model folder. Escape in the field moves the focus to the conversation and keeps the text; Enter that confirms an input method's candidate sends nothing.
    • Built on 26 October 2026 on the branch amd-07/composer under amendment 07 of model interface specification 0.4, AI/build-areas/model-interface/spec_0.4/amendments/amd-07-the-composer-of-the-matsya-column.md, merge pending AAS: field.vue's property grows, the handle composer of window.vue, the composer of matsya_window.py and its rules in brand.css; the tests read the composer as a text area again, and packages/bellman-workbench/tests/test_amd_07.py checks the amendment's six cases.     2026-10-26 — The value wire carries its derivatives¶

    • A marginal value is no longer a field of a backward header or a wire of the builders graph. The derivative of a value is read where a line needs it by the derivative term D_{a}V[>], the operator D applied to the value field written with its perch mark, the subscript a state coordinate of the perch at which the value is read, written without its mark; a backward header lists value fields and the controls at which its lines are read, and nothing else. The roles !mrgnvl and !sndmrgnvl, the naming convention dV, d_<coordinate><value> and the refusal marginal-value-pairing are retired, and a header that writes a retired role word is refused with a sentence naming the term (Stages; Field equations §2; Expression forms; Refusals).
    • A derivative line, D_{m_d}V = 𝑑𝑢(c), in a value builder's block of the stage file or in a marginal block bound with !marginals, states the derivative of the value the builder writes. methodize records it at the builder's Evaluation beside the representation method, by coordinate with its id and source; the graph gains no wire and no box, the builder's signature is unchanged, the value's record lists the coordinate under derivatives, and stage.obligations() lists the reading under marginal-reading. How a derivative is evaluated is a property of the value fixed where it is built, by the stated line, by the value's representation, or by a representation of the stated derivative bound on the derivative line's id, on: cntn_to_dcsn.envelope; a block that reads D_{a}V[>] declares nothing about it. No derivative line is written on an arrival builder whose law between the perches is the identity, where the stored derivative values pass through; where the law is not the identity, the author writes the arrival derivative line with the law's factor (Methodization §4.1).
    • The method table loses the column "reads a marginal value" and gains derivatives it evaluates, stated for !Linear, !Cartesian and !pchip, and the condition marginal-reading of !marginals reads as the derivative line's assertion under the envelope conditions (Method types). bellman.trellis.derivative_missing(trellis) replaces marginal_missing and answers one derivative-missing diagnostic for each derivative reading no route meets, naming the stage, the value and the coordinate; the join check compares value fields alone (Trellises §2.1). A row that reads a derivative term compiles to no kernel and is refused under kernel-form.
    • The explicit way of writing stays, with derivative terms in place of marginal fields, and a stage file may define dV = D_{a}V[>] and read the name. The canonical example, applications/cons-port-demonstration/, writes its states unindexed by perch, m at the decision perch, and declares no arrival perch: its decision value is the value the stage exports, and the trellis joins the stage before it at its decision state. The Bellman Workbench draws a value as one wire, whose port's hover lists the derivatives the value carries, prints the stated derivative on a card at the Evaluation beside the representation, and shows derivative-missing under the Problems mark (The Workbench). The pages of the documentation, the catalogues of the calculus and the example pages state the amended language.
    • Matsya's configuration 0.3.32, ID fe160391c16327681b3f564f423898ead4254f47331eed7985c2095568603356, revises the instruction texts of the Bellman-SYM writer, the source preparer, the paper-form reader and the typed-form writer for the derivative term in place of the marginal field and regenerates the three saved guides from the pages of this entry; the Matsya handler, the settings and the retrieval index are unchanged.
    • Built on 26 October 2026 by the units of the plan AI/build-areas/Bellman-Sym/spec_0.6/2026-10-26-amd-mb-001-implementation-plan.md, the language, methodization and the trellis, the applications, the pages and the Workbench, Matsya's texts and guides, and the tests, and merged into main the same day by the merge commit that carries this entry; the cons-port demonstration reproduces its reference in both orders to 1e-13, and the Workbench draws its consumption stage with values alone and its operative builders with the stated derivative at the Evaluation. The retrieval index is not rebuilt and the writer job of the amendment's item 6 is not run; both wait for AAS.

    2026-10-22 — Method blocks and marginal blocks¶

    • A methods file may name a block file under the new key block, a path from the methods file's folder, and methodize reads it. A method block, whose header carries the signature of a builder of the stage, becomes that builder's operations, as the block of !egm does on the policy builder of a stage file that writes the maximization alone; a marginal block, bound by the new method !marginals on a backward value builder, appends the author's marginal lines to that builder, whose Evaluation then writes the marginal value beside the value. Nothing is differentiated, and the stage file is not changed (Methodization §4.1; Expression forms §4).
    • The binding verbs calibrate, methodize and configure add their binding to the stage they are called on and return it; nothing is copied. stage.declared reads the builders as the stage file states them and stage.builder the operative ones, the same builders until a block is bound, and the initial diagrams and the declared builders are the same objects before and after every binding. A target holds one binding of each kind, a solution method, a representation or a marginal block, and a later binding of a kind replaces the earlier (The binding verbs).
    • Each method type of the package's method table states whether it takes a block, whether it reads a marginal value, and the condition its construction requires, which stage.obligations() lists and nothing checks, egm-eligibility for !egm and marginal-reading for !marginals. !time-iteration takes a block and reads a marginal value, and !vfi reads the setting n_c, the number of points of the finite feasible set its search uses (Method types).
    • bellman.trellis.marginal_missing(trellis) reports a marginal value that a method reads and the stage supplying the continuation does not export, as a marginal-missing diagnostic naming the stage and the field; nothing is refused (Trellises §2.1).
    • A block file stands at declaration/methods/<name>.bl of a model folder (Model directory layout §4). The folder applications/cons-port-demonstration/ binds the consumption stage's block file cons_egm.bl and the portfolio stage's port_foc.bl in both orders of its trellis.
    • The encoding schema admits the key block in a method entry, so that the encoding of a stage bound with a block file passes validate_encoding. The notebook applications/cons-port-demonstration/notebooks/declared-and-operative.ipynb shows the declared and the operative builders of the demonstration's two stages, and the marginal value each order of its trellis requires, met and then unmet.
    • The demonstration applications/cons-port-demonstration/ writes the consumption and portfolio stages with their maximization alone and binds the endogenous grid method as a method block, with the marginal utility and its inverse written in closed form in the block file; its two orders, consumption first and portfolio first, reproduce the cons-port reference to 1e-12 (tests/test_reference_solve.py). The cons-port application's own recipes bind that consumption stage, and lessons 01, 03 and 04 of the cons-port tutorials are recorded again on it.
    • Bellman Workbench: the button Operative builders at the foot of a stage's graph, beside Perch overlays, switches the builders shown and the sections of Details between the declared builders and the builders a method opens, off when a folder opens (design pass 15, Workbench); a node names every method its box holds.
    • Bellman Workbench: the Matsya column stands on the page's ground behind one hairline, with a composer box of context chips, the text field and a foot row holding the kind of request, its output level or collection and the send control (design pass 16); a question from a connected folder is a turn of its project session, a build shows its prepared material before it is sent and its result in five parts, citations open their file and line, their saved copy or their passage, and returned files are downloaded into keyed copies, inspected read-only under their own root and adopted through a recorded, recoverable plan (amendment 06 of the model-interface specification).

    2026-10-21 — Matsya fixes a job's source material at acceptance, and the client's four additions (specification 0.6, unit G1)¶

    • A job from a session may name reviewed_through, the number of the last session entry its user reviewed. The job store checks the session in the transaction that accepts the job: entries after that number refuse it with 409 and a sentence naming each entry by its number and kind, a number beyond the last entry refuses it with 422, and neither creates a job. Otherwise the store fixes the job's source material, its kind, the revision, the entries' numbers and the earlier job it continues, the session's latest job at acceptance; the worker reads them in place of the session and passes that earlier job's record to the processor, so that neither an entry added nor a job ended while the job waits changes what it reads; the job's answer names reviewed_through; and the record names the history it was built from under captured_source, which the products view keeps (packages/matsya/matsya_service/jobs.py, check_reviewed_history; processor.py, check_request). A job submitted without the field is built as before. A job request may name a delivery_id: the same request sent again under it returns the job held, its present state and repeated true, and creates nothing, and a different request under it is refused with 409. A repeated delivery_id on a reply or a turn already returned the entry or the turn held, with no scheduled value and no new work (AMD-MAT-006 §8 rule 3), and is unchanged.
    • The migration adds to the jobs table the columns reviewed_through INTEGER, captured_source TEXT, continues_job TEXT and delivery_id TEXT, with an index that keeps a delivery_id unique among one principal's jobs, NULL in every row written before it, so that every earlier job reads as a job submitted without either field; no other table changes, and the record schemas matsya-model-iteration/7 and /6 are unchanged, captured_source being an optional entry of a captured job's record. The answer of GET /v1/index names capabilities, ["capture_at_acceptance", "job_delivery_id"], and service_version, so that a client claims either only of a service that names it. The Matsya service is version 0.1.13. A client released before this change sends no reviewed_through, and every answer it reads keeps its fields.
    • The client bellman-matsya is version 0.13.0: job submit-prepared <file> [--delivery-id ID] submits a job from a prepared-inputs file (the session, the last entry reviewed, the target, the starting declaration as submit_job takes it and each file's path in the model folder; bellman_matsya.client.read_prepared_inputs defines it) and is refused, before the job request, by a service whose index answer does not name the capability; configuration effective prints the service address, the configuration, the session and the sources of the Matsya token and the personal key, without either secret, and reports a key the client does not send to the address as refused; ask --no-wait prints a turn's identifier at once and ask --delivery-id names its delivery; and turn status and turn wait read a turn by its identifier from any later process (packages/bellman-matsya-client/src/bellman_matsya/).
    • Built and tested locally on the branch spec06/matsya-service (packages/matsya/tests/test_spec_0_6_unit_g1.py, packages/bellman-matsya-client/tests/test_spec_0_6_unit_g1_client.py; decisions in AI/road-map/issues-to-be-addressed/spec-06-decisions-made-by-ai/SG1-unit-g1-the-service-and-the-client.md).     2026-10-21 — The comparator integrates a uniform law¶

    • The comparator of packages/meaning-map-training integrates Uniform(a, b) by Gauss–Legendre nodes on [a, b], the weights summing to one and a polynomial of the draw exact up to degree 2n − 1 at n nodes, so that an expectation over a uniform shock is evaluated at its test points as one over a normal or a lognormal shock is; endpoints equal, reversed or infinite are refused as outside the domain, and TruncatedNormal and TypeIExtremeValue are still not integrated (compare.py, _Evaluator.law_nodes). CDC asked for it on 20 October 2026, after the cross-toolkit schemas' converter wrote Uniform(0.6, 1.4) and the comparator reported the law not integrated. The list of law families the comparator keeps apart from the names of a stage now names Uniform, TruncatedNormal and TypeIExtremeValue, the families of pull request 17, as the package's table of families does. Tests: tests/test_stage_compare.py, the uniform's nodes and moments, an identity that holds under a declared uniform law only, and a pair that differs by the family.

    2026-10-21 — The comparator evaluates finite index sets, indexed data and category codes¶

    • The comparator of the meaning-map training evaluates at its test points the kernels that read the forms of pull requests 14 and 16, which it did not evaluate before, so that two writings differing in such a kernel, dist[i_d][j] for dist[j][i_d], ended undecided and not differ (packages/meaning-map-training/meaning_map_training/compare.py, _draw and the functions it calls). A name declared on an integer range, i : {1, …, J}, is drawn as an integer between the bounds of the range at the point, a parameter that a bound reads, J, being drawn first from its own declared set where the point lacks it; a name declared on a listed set, {0, θ} ⊂ ℤ, takes one of its members; and a name declared on a comprehension over the integers, the category-coded {i : ℤ | i >= 0, i <= 1}, takes an integer between the bounds its conditions write, drawn again until every condition holds, so that the code 0 is drawn as well as the code 1.
    • A name declared on a set of functions, δ : L_J → ℝ or dist : L_J × L_J → ℝ₊, is drawn as a table with one entry drawn from the codomain for each member of the index set, each entry drawn on its own, so that a table on pairs is in general not symmetric; the table on a product is nested by its factors, and a table on a set of labels is keyed by the positions at which a field on that set is drawn. The selections δ[j] and dist[j][i_d] read the entry at the integer key nearest the selector, a missing key making the point invalid; the selection from a tuple is unchanged (_Evaluator.value). A table whose index set cannot be listed at a point, among them a listed set whose parameter member is drawn as no integer, makes the point invalid for a statement that reads it and is left out of the point for a statement that does not (_points). A table lies in its set of functions and in no other set (_within), and a witness writes a drawn table with its keys as text (_rounded).
    • The items print the three set forms as a stage file spells them, {1, …, J}, L_J × L_J → ℝ₊ and {0, θ} ⊂ ℤ, where before they printed the fields of the term, IntegerRange(lower=1, upper=J) (packages/meaning-map-training/meaning_map_training/items.py, term_text).
    • Six tests of packages/meaning-map-training/tests/test_stage_compare.py check the change on the location stage of packages/bellman/tests/test_finite_indexed_data.py with J : ℤ, >= 1, on its writing with the outside option I_J := {0, …, J}, on the household stage of packages/bellman/tests/test_category_codes.py and on the stage with listed integer keys; at the base b3df18733 five of them fail, four ending undecided where differ is required and one on the printed text. The harness suite gives 510 passed and 36 skipped, with one failure that stands at the base, test_round_trip.py::test_anonymise_hides_the_name_and_the_path_of_the_portfolio_cons_dossier, whose stage file of commit a8b484899 the compiler now refuses under input-perch-state. The 80 stage files that elaborate under applications/, packages/bellman/tests/corpus/ and the harness's fixtures, each compared with itself and with its mutations, 918 comparisons in all, give the same profiles as at the base. An expectation over a shock with one of the three laws of pull request 17 is still not evaluated, since the quadrature integrates the normal and lognormal laws and their mixtures only.
    • The package meaning-map-training is version 0.1.8, and the Matsya service is 0.1.12 and requires it. mutate.py, the round-trip programs and the Bellman package are unchanged.

    2026-10-21 — The proposer's classification rule¶

The branching clause of item 1 and item 3 of the forms the meaning-map training found missing had entered that list without such a refusal (AAS, 21 October 2026). The rule applies from the next epoch's copy of the text; no epoch in progress is changed.

    2026-10-21 — One block for alternatives with one successor, and the death branch's value¶

    • Branching §1 and Stages §2.1 state that alternatives which all lead to the same successor stage are not branches: the choice is an integer control over the index set, and one block retains the chosen alternative as the integer continuation state, i_n = j, so that the number of alternatives stays a parameter; an alternative with a continuation problem of its own is a branch of a separate stage. Branching §4 states that a branch on which the model ends keeps the state its terminal value reads, and that the stage supplying that value lists V[>], which the user supplies at realization, zero where the paper gives no bequest value (AAS, 21 October 2026). No compiler code changed.
