    2026-09-07 — Appendix C rewritten over the stage file: fields, contexts, and the lowered function graph¶

    • docs/Bellman-Sym/appendices/14-appendix-c-syntax-to-semantics.md: rewritten from the stage-YAML carrier to the .bl stage file, keeping the file name and the rule identifiers R1 to R12. The page now states each rule over the written expressions and the declared typing context: what a name denotes (a field, a random variable measurable at its perch), which function of the declared state represents it, and how the functions compose into the stage's backward and forward operators. Perch form and functional form are two readings of one context, and no notation for random variables against realisations is introduced. Three earlier claims are withdrawn on the page: that a perch mark is a binder (R4; a mark asserts measurability, and V[>] in a value statement is free), that a numerical method's need creates an operator (R7, R11; only !node does), and that choosing the endogenous grid method inserts evaluate and upper_env into the stage's reading (R12; the envelope is the maximisation over the fibre of the coordinate map, authored or performed inside the method). R10 states the relation a policy block determines with or without its final line and the premises under which the two agree, and states the complementarity clause ⟂ as a mixed complementarity condition. Worked derivations cover a product-output stage without consumption, cons.bl and cons_savings_iid.bl with their discount conventions, the paired perch-form and functional-form derivation of V[<] = E_{y}(V), a constrained policy, the endogenous-grid policy with and without its final line, and the retirement choice of ret_choice.bl with the pension model. Every complete example parses under the current stage grammar. Parser, program, and library vocabulary is kept off the page; internal paths and version tokens are removed.

    2026-09-07 — Bellman calculus terminology¶

    • Use Bellman calculus in place of T-calculus. The working note now describes mathematical objects, operations and their connections directly, without naming a separate “core” layer. Its former path links to the renamed note so historical references remain usable. The mathematical rules are unchanged.

    2026-09-06 — The optional decision-perch mark [~]¶

    • packages/bellman/bellman/syntax/grammars/stage.lark, lower.py: x[~] is accepted wherever x[<] and x[>] are (expressions, signature coordinates, declaration and law subjects) and marks the decision perch; a field at that perch is written bare, so x[~] lowers to the same term as x and a file's JSON encoding does not depend on whether the mark is written. The lowering does not check that a marked name is at the decision perch: the perch of a bare name is that of the signature side listing it. The tilde is the ASCII form of the subscript \(\sim\) of the mathematics. In dyno's own files k[~] is the steady-state marker; a stage has no steady state, and the stage grammar gives the bracket tilde this meaning instead (AAS, 6 September 2026). Pages 01, 12, and 13 of docs/Bellman-Sym/ and reference/symbols-conventions.md list the mark; tests in packages/bellman/tests/sym/test_decision_perch_mark.py.
    • applications/consumption-savings-iid/cons_savings_iid.bl: the endogenous-grid statement is now [id=g_cntn_to_dcsn] (the map from the continuation state back to the decision state) and the implicit statement is written c[~] = c[>]; derived encodings regenerated, the page beside it updated.

    2026-09-05 — Three views of a stage; operators are occurrences, not nodes; the tower is a realisation tower¶

    • docs/Bellman-Sym/appendices/14-appendix-c-syntax-to-semantics.md R1, R7, R10: a line becomes a node only when it carries @node (!node in the .bl surface). A numerical operator inside a line — E, argmax, solve, upper_env — is an operator occurrence, a box only at the finest of three views of one stage (builder view; node view, each builder cut by its @node marks; finest view, one box per occurrence), and the methods file's targets choose which view the solver works at. Methods attach to a builder box, a node box, or an operator occurrence, and to nothing else; a method named by operator governs every occurrence in the stage. R10's inversion line is a solve occurrence, a node only when marked. Untagged lines that define a block output are the builder's boundary statements; alias is reserved for an intermediate name folded into a later line. Decided by AAS, 4–5 September 2026 (spec 0.3c decision register, D06).
    • docs/bellman-calculus/APPX-F-OPERATOR-ALGEBRA.md §F.6: "the graded refinement tower" is renamed the graded realisation tower, and its laws speak of each move of the tower; the convergence net is a settings net. "Refine" now means moving to a finer view of a stage (as in R7 and Appendix B) and is not used for the lifecycle, whose steps are methodize, configure, and calibrate. The wiki entry operator follows.

    2026-07-18 — The carrier is exchangeable¶

    • docs/bellman-calculus/11-abstract-grammar.md §1: a paragraph added stating that the per-construct encoding — the head, then the keys' values in schema order — is the whole contract a file format must deliver. YAML is the canonical carrier, inherited from the dolo lineage; an alternative carrier is an adapter onto the same tree, changing a model's spelling and never its meaning. The ruling and the precedent survey behind it are recorded in the project road-map.

    2026-07-18 — Stage page: abstract-grammar box; the perch state groups renamed¶

    • docs/Bellman-Sym/01-stage.md §1 completed with the normative Abstract grammar: STAGE box, replacing the placeholder. The box fixes the head (!stage, name, optional kind) and the full symbols: block; the equations and dolo_plus sorts are carried opaque (the equations grammar belongs to the Field-equations page). The continuation-states group takes Flat | Branched alternatives, so a fork is read from the block's shape; decorator payloads are terminal atoms, per the no-AST position. The Status paragraph records the two software deviations (the kind: branching requirement; the legacy group names in the loaders).
    • The perch state declaration groups are renamed (decided 18 Jul 2026): arvl_states / dcsn_states / cntn_states, replacing prestate / states / poststates, which parse as permanent aliases. The grouped declaration shape is confirmed — the ruling and its reasons (the abstract syntax tree must carry enough authored context to build the semantic graph deterministically) are recorded in the road-map issue brief. The page's grammar box, group table, and examples carry the new spelling; the loader, translator, schema reference, and example files follow.
    • Smaller corrections on the same page: the summary box now names both aggregation equations — \(G_{\succ}\) (cntn_to_dcsn) and \(G_{\prec}\) (dcsn_to_arvl) — where it previously denied the arrival edge an aggregator; a note records that the feasible set has no declaration of its own beyond the @in domains (open design question, tracked in the road-map); page properties (front matter) added, matching the period and trellis pages.
    • Declarations read unquoted (decided 18 Jul 2026): the language's spelling is w: @in R+; the quoted form (w: "@in R+") remains what today's loaders require — standard YAML reserves @ at the start of an unquoted value — and is what generated files emit. Recorded as a software difference in the stage page's grammar box until the reader accepts the unquoted form.

    2026-07-18 — Value symbols are fields; the "perch value objects" section retired¶

The tag-function table row updated to match.

    2026-07-16 — New theory page: Abstract grammar¶

    • New page docs/bellman-calculus/11-abstract-grammar.md ("11. Abstract grammar" in the nav): the stage, period, and trellis files read as sentences of the calculus. The nesting of the authored objects is presented as the language's abstract grammar (its abstract syntax): a constructor grammar bottoming out at the modeller's data — transitions, rewards, shock distributions, parameters — with the builders produced by the Υ evaluation rather than present in the term. Periods and trellises are insert-composition of represented stage objects in Backus's FFP sense, with the FP-versus-FFP licensing of that fold stated precisely; the page closes with what the tree is not (no parse of value-level arithmetic; a shared term graph at run time; many-sorted and fixed-vocabulary where Backus is untyped and open-ended).
    • The draft was checked against the Backus 1978 chunk file and the corpus by a Matsya literature query and two independent adversarial reviews; every confirmed finding was applied before publication.

    2026-06-15 — Operator algebra (Appendix F) + methods catalogue, validated against the applications¶

    • New Appendix F — Operator algebra (docs/bellman-calculus/APPX-F-OPERATOR-ALGEBRA.md): the algebra the operators form — the presented distributive category (cartesian backward / Markov forward), branching by coproducts, the graded refinement tower (mathematize → parameterize → methodize → settingize → horse), and the operator type system (morphisms typed by rank × grade). Hardened against adversarial review by two Matsya models (opus 4.8 and gpt-5.5 pro), which converged on: the carrier is a presented distributive category (not a free SMC); branching needs coproducts; the methodization law is mathematization-preserving with convergence (\(\rho\to\Upsilon\)), not denotation-erasure (this supersedes the §3.4 erasure reading of spec 0.1a); "transpose under the pairing," scoped to the policy-fixed linear fragment, replaces "conjugate."
    • Methods catalogue (Appendix E.5–E.6, catalogues/methods.md, catalogues/method-schemes.md): single source of truth for methods. Methods attach to operators directly — the legacy scheme: classifier is retired (migration table in E.5). Supersedes dolo-plus-spec/reference/method-schemes.md and the methods/operations sections of primitives.md.
    • Operator catalogue (E.3) expanded to the complete inventory (structural morphisms, branching operators, implicit/auxiliary operators, the gradient and multi-control solve codomains); the "operators are not typed" wording corrected to "typed by their rank but not inhabitants of a sort."
    • Validated against the applications (cons-port, consumption-savings-iid, housing-owner, durables, mortgages, retirement/unemployment branching, benhabib-2019): catalogue + theory cover the apps; fixes folded in (!golden-section, !endogenous grid, !scalar-multiply, branching aggregators max_J/E_J, the i.i.d.-mixture vs persistent-regime split, the NEGM-vs-DCEGM distinction, the random-growth/ergodic out-of-scope note). Review artifacts under AI/working/AAS/14062026/.

    2026-06-11 — Forward-builder notation: \(\mathbb{F}_\prec \to \mathbb{F}\), \(\mathbb{F}^{\sigma}_{\succ} \to \mathbb{G}_{\pi}\)¶

    • The decision-edge forward builder is now written \(\mathbb{G}_{\pi}\) — a pushforward under a policy function \(\pi\) — and the arrival-edge forward builder is plain \(\mathbb{F}\) (no subscript). Rationale: the decision-side object exists only once a policy fills the action slot, so the notation should name the policy rather than the perch; with the two builders distinguished by letter, neither needs a \(\prec\)/\(\succ\) subscript. The composite forward stage operator is \(\mathbb{F}^{\pi} = \mathbb{G}_{\pi} \circ \mathbb{F}\) (previously \(\mathbb{F}^{\sigma}\)), and the policy symbol in forward contexts is \(\pi\) throughout (the conjugacy partner is the policy-closed expectation \(\mathbb{E}^{\pi}_{\eta}\)); \(\sigma\) remains in backward-side contexts. Blackboard \(\mathbb{G}\) is distinct from the roman aggregator \(\mathrm{G}\) of the stage tuple.
    • Updated: docs/bellman-calculus/ (02, 03, 04, 05, APPX-B), docs/dolo-plus-spec/reference/symbols-conventions.md, AI/rules/terminology.md (forward-builder row), the wiki (forward_builder page and schema description), and the live specs (semantics 0.0 and 0.1a, rosetta-stone 0.2, forward-builders 0.1m — where the old plain-\(\mathbb{F}\) composite is now \(\mathbb{F}^{\pi}\) to free \(\mathbb{F}\) for the arrival builder). Schema-level symbols F_ad / F_de are unchanged; the math column of the 0.1m mapping table records the correspondence \(\mathbb{F} \leftrightarrow\) F_ad, \(\mathbb{G}_{\pi} \leftrightarrow\) F_de. Historical material (superseded specs, docs/old/, the ModularMDP-era unified sections, past presentation decks) retains the old notation as a record.

    2026-04-30 — The multi-period composite renamed from nest to trellis¶

    • Multi-period composite renamed: nest → trellis (terminology lock): The object that bellman-ddsl previously called nest (and that SolvingMicroDSOPs called pile) is now canonically the trellis. Both prior names were placeholders without literature precedent; trellis has a precise meaning in coding theory (Forney 1973, "The Viterbi Algorithm") and is used by Bertsekas (DP and Optimal Control Vol I, Ch. 2) for finite-state DP visualisations. Decided jointly by AAS and CDC after a Matsya-led literature search. See AI/working/AAS/30042026/session.md for the full rationale and docs/dolo-plus-spec/reference/definitions.md for the canonical entry.
      • Phase 1 (this release) — canonical references and the active artefact under mod/benhabib/:
        • docs/dolo-plus-spec/reference/definitions.md: added "Trellis" entry.
        • docs/dolo-plus-spec/reference/terminology.md: 6 references updated.
        • AI/dev-specs/dolang+/spec_0.1/spec_0.1-overview.md: milestone 0.1h description updated.
        • docs/examples/Benhabib_et_al_2019/mod/benhabib/: nest.yaml → trellis.yaml; README updated.
      • Phase 2 (deferred) — the Python module and example YAMLs:
        • packages/dolo/dolo/compiler/nest_factory/ → trellis_factory/ (with deprecation alias).
        • packages/dolo/examples/models/doloplus/*/nest.yaml and explore/*/nest.yaml: rename with the parser still accepting both names.
        • mkdocs.yml nav references; docs/development/dolo-plus-impl/spec_0.1h-periods-nests.md content. Phase 2 needs a focused commit because the Python module rename requires a deprecation alias and parser-side dual-name support; not safely combined with Phase 1.

    2026-03-21 — The decision perch's subscript changed from ∘ to ∼, and the Matsya prompt corrected¶

    • Decision-perch subscript: \(\circ\) → \(\sim\) (repo-wide): Adopted \sim as the canonical math subscript for the decision perch, replacing \circ. This aligns bellman-ddsl with SolvingMicroDSOPs notation and frees \circ for its standard use as function composition. Updated across all spec pages, theory docs, examples, formal MDPs, dev-specs, working notes, and the Matsya system prompt.
      • symbols-conventions.md: perch index table, filtration definition, Rosetta Stone, all transition subscripts (\(\mathrm{g}_{\prec\sim}\), \(\mathrm{g}_{\sim\succ}\))
      • Syntax-semantic-rules: 01, 06, 07, 08
      • Theory: 03-symbol-system, 05-bellman-theory, 06-representation-maps, 07-execution-pipeline, MDP-foundations/index, dolo-sym-to-ddsl-core-mapping
      • Examples: solving-conjugates, unemployment/index, formal MDPs (noport_stage_mdp, unemployment_branching_mdp)
      • Dev-specs: spec_0.1l-branching-theory, spec_0.1eA-stage-operators-to-dolo
      • References: ddsl_schema, dolo-to-doloplus-syntax-changes
      • AI/working: dolo-plus.md, T-calc-core.md, session notes, perch-index exploration
      • Matsya prompt (Bellman-DDSL.md): transition notation, branching notation
      • \(\sim\) over \(\circ\) for decision perch: Using \sim eliminates ambiguity with function composition (\mathbb{I} \circ \mathbb{B}). The tilde visually suggests "approximately here" or "the reference point" — the decision perch is the unmarked default from which \prec and \succ are defined. SolvingMicroDSOPs already uses \sim; adopting it in bellman-ddsl unifies the notation across the two repositories.
    • Matsya system prompt fixes (HIGH + MEDIUM severity):
      • max_{c}() vs max_{c}{}: now accepts both; braces are canonical for operator bodies, parentheses accepted for named-operator arguments
      • ShadowBellman: removed false "DEPRECATED" claim — ShadowBellman is valid in dcsn_to_arvl_builder; MarginalBellman is valid in cntn_to_dcsn_builder
      • [~] decision tag: softened prohibition — unmarked is preferred, [~] accepted as explicit marker
      • Unicode parameters: softened mandate — both β/ρ and beta/rho are valid
      • _opr alias: noted as valid synonym for _builder (used by canonical consumption_savings_iid example)
      • V_cntn branching notation: replaced with spec 0.1l canonical V[>][label] sugar (looked up via values.V[>] map)

    2026-02-12 — Specification 0.2a of the compiler pipeline, and the perch tags normalized¶

    • spec 0.2a (compiler pipeline architecture): Lean 4-inspired architectural spec for the full dolo-plus compiler pipeline.
      • AI/dev-specs/dolang+/spec_0.2/spec_0.2a-compiler-pipeline-architecture.md
      • 8-stage pipeline (Parse → Validate → Υ → Methodize → Calibrate → Ρ → Solve → Analyze) with typed intermediate representations at each stage.
      • Operator registry with attribute-based registration (pluggable methods as Lean-style tactics).
      • Immutable environment threading (scoped extensions pattern).
      • Template-driven Ρ-map code generation.
      • Multi-error validation following Lean's collect-all-errors approach.
      • Composition pipeline design for periods and nests.
      • Pluggable methods as tactics: Lean's tactic framework (registered proof strategies dispatched by name) maps directly to the DDSL operator registry (registered solver methods dispatched by method tag). The @register_method decorator pattern enables extensibility without modifying core pipeline code.
      • Persistent IRs prevent black-box pipelines: Every pipeline stage should produce a serializable, inspectable intermediate representation. This makes debugging, diffing, and testing tractable — you can inspect a methodized stage without running calibration.
      • Immutable environment threading: Following Lean's scoped extensions, each pipeline stage should extend (never mutate) the symbol/method environment. This is especially important for composition, where each stage in a period has its own scope joined by connector renames.
    • Lean 4 ≈ CORE elaboration note: added analogy connecting Lean 4's elaboration pipeline to the φ (SYM → CORE) translation in 08-sym-core-architecture.md, for-engineers.md, and homepage.
      • Elaboration ≈ φ (SYM → CORE): Lean 4's elaboration (surface Syntax → kernel Expr) is the precise analogue of the Bellman-calculus φ map (dolo-plus YAML → normalized typed stage IR). The kernel/elaboration separation principle means CORE should be small and trusted; all complexity lives in the SYM-to-CORE desugaring.
    • AI/matsya.md: added Matsya RAG configuration file to repo.
    • Perch tag propagation (repo-wide):
      • Parser (packages/dolang/dolang/grammar.py): added normalize_perch_glyphs() pre-parse step — [<]/[>] glyph tags normalize to canonical [_arvl]/[_cntn] before Lark parsing.
      • Parser (packages/dolo/dolo/compiler/doloplus_translator/transforms.py): defensive normalization in perch_to_time() and shift_perch_tags().
      • MDP foundations (docs/theory/MDP-foundations/index.md): full notation rewrite — subscript → superscript perch indices (\(x_{\prec}\) → \(x^{\prec}\)), transition functions (\(\mathrm{g}_{\prec}/\mathrm{g}_{\succ}\) → \(\mathrm{f}/\mathrm{g}\)), preferred SYM tags ([<]/unmarked/[>]).
      • Homepage, for-engineers, language guide, dolo-dynare-relationship, stage-structure-and-timing: updated to preferred perch tags and superscript notation.
    • Canonical naming: "Dolo+" → "dolo-plus": global rename across 51 files (387 occurrences). Updated CLAUDE.md canonical terminology table accordingly.
    • Value object perch tags: V → V[-] and dV → dV[-] in all YAML snippets where decision-perch value objects appeared bare. Value objects that exist at all three perches require explicit [-] tag at the decision perch (unlike controls/states where unmarked = decision by convention). Updated in homepage, symbols-conventions, for-engineers, consumption_savings_iid example doc, ddsl_foundations proposal.
      • Unmarked ≠ bare for value objects: The "unmarked = decision" convention works for controls and states (which live at exactly one perch), but value objects (V, dV) that span multiple perches must always carry an explicit perch tag. This is a measurability distinction: V[-] asserts "V evaluated at the decision σ-algebra", which differs from V[<] and V[>].

    2026-02-12 — The semiconjugate solve of specification 0.1eA, with the fixes and reviews around it¶

    • Semiconjugate solve (spec 0.1eA implementation): full pipeline from dolo-plus split-file stages through accretive nest build to EGM solve with Stachurski's semiconjugate transform.
      • explore/semiconjugate_solve.py — three solve methods (egm black-box, egm per-period, manual loop), all verified to exact match.
      • explore/semiconjugate_solve.ipynb — hand-holdy notebook with Stachurski references, iterates lemma, per-cell math explanations.
    • return_internals for egm() (dolo): returns raw sa0/xa0/z arrays alongside EGMResult, enabling per-period EGM calls without cubic-resampling error accumulation.
    • make_egm_dr0_from_internals() (dolo): builds a linear-interpolation dr0 callable from raw EGM internals — matches what egm() uses between its own internal iterations.
    • mapping_set() (dolang): yaml_nodes.mapping_set(m, key, value_node) for setting/updating keys in YAML MappingNode data.
    • spec_0.1p (intermediate-variable indexing): placeholder spec for c[_a], m_d[_a] bracket syntax — intermediate variables as local perch indices within builder sub-equations.
      • AI/dev-specs/dolang+/spec_0.1/0.1_p/spec_0.1p-intermediate-variable-indexing.md
    • Deliverables addendum: docs/deliverables-AAS-Feb2026.md — polished Feb–Apr 2026 SOW addendum with tags and repo links.
    • Model.set_calibration() (dolo): now works with MappingNode data from yaml.compose(). Previously crashed with TypeError: 'MappingNode' object is not subscriptable.
    • solving-conjugates.md: front-to-back review — fixed Ŝ vs S operator labelling, rewrote pseudocode to use real repo APIs (doloplus_to_dolo, egm()), added "level values vs EGM objects" conceptual seam, removed nonexistent helpers.
    • spec_0.1eA: fixed β-placement convention (β in direct_response_egm, not expectation), added import numpy, renamed Step 4 to reflect mr(a) output.
    • noport_stage_mdp.md: \mathrm{T} → \mathbf{T}, E → \mathbf{E}.
    • CLAUDE.md: added single-changelog rule (no root CHANGELOG.md).
